Day 237 - Morning

Ardan's Watch — The Foundations

w_mute




Alexiel



Mage



scavenger

Long ago, in the world of LARION, Alexiel stands before the iron gates of Riverside Manor as a cold, gray dawn breaks over the Silverflow river.

The half-elf’s fine linen robes feel thin against the biting morning air. A heavy silence hangs over the valley, broken only by the sluggish rush of the nearby water and the crunch of dead leaves beneath her boots.

The manor house is a shell of its former self. Gray stone walls remain sturdy, but the slate roof is pockmarked with holes, and the tall windows look like hollow eyes.

Weeds have choked the gravel drive, and the salmon-carved fountain in the center of the courtyard sits dry, its stone basin cracked and filled with stagnant rainwater.

The heavy oak doors of the manor creak open with a groan of rusted hinges. A man steps out, leaning heavily on a cane.

His clothes were once fine livery, now threadbare and patched at the elbows.

OLD STEWARD

"So... a Tethlar finally returns to claim the rot."

OLD STEWARD

"I wasn't sure the letters would ever find you, Lady Alexiel."

He hobbles down the stairs, his eyes scanning Alexiel’s sharp features and the dark-stained wooden staff she grips. He stops a few paces away, his breath hitching in his chest.

OLD STEWARD

"I am Barnaby. I’ve been keeping the rats and the rain out as best I can for five years now, though the rain is winning."

OLD STEWARD

"There’s no coin left, my Lady. Not since your uncle..."

OLD STEWARD

"well, since the tragedy."

Barnaby gestures toward the neighboring hill, where a much larger, well-kept estate gleams even in the dull light.

OLD STEWARD

"Lady Ashbury’s been circling like a hawk. She sent her man again yesterday with another pittance of an offer to buy the deeds."

OLD STEWARD

"Says a ruined house is a blight on the valley."

The old man coughs into a rag, then looks up at Alexiel with a flicker of desperate hope in his clouded eyes.

OLD STEWARD

"The tenants in Willowford are starving and the vineyard is a jungle of thorns. They need a master."

OLD STEWARD

"Or a mistress. Will you come inside and see the damage for yourself?"

Turn 0

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Continue

Barnaby turns back to the heavy oak doors, his shoulder leaning into the wood to force them wider.

The groan of the metal hinges echoes across the empty courtyard. He stops to catch his breath, his thin chest heaving, then gestures toward the dark maw of the entry hall with a trembling hand.

BARNABY

"The Grand Hall is a tragedy of dust and cobwebs, my Lady, but the slate above it is sound enough. At least the portraits won't drown tonight."

He steps over the threshold, his cane clicking rhythmically against the cold stone floor. Inside, the air smells of damp earth and old wax.

Shafts of gray morning light pierce the gloom, revealing mounds of furniture shrouded in moth-eaten sheets.

BARNABY

"I have kept the ledger in the study. It is a grim read—mostly red ink and broken promises—but it is honest."

BARNABY

"There is also the matter of the family silver. I've kept the last of the service hidden beneath the floorboards in the pantry to keep it from the debt collectors."

He pauses at the base of a wide, sweeping staircase that leads into the shadows of the upper floor. A piece of banister lies shattered on the steps, and a stray draft stirs the heavy velvet curtains hanging lopsided over the windows.

BARNABY

"If you have the stomach for it, we should start with the books. Knowing exactly how much we owe the world is better than guessing in the dark."

The old man waits for Alexiel to follow, his posture straightening as if the mere presence of a Tethlar in the house has given his weary bones a momentary spark of strength.

Turn 1

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Continue

Alexiel follows the old man deeper into the hall, her leather armor creaking softly in the silence.

The amber gem atop her staff pulses with a faint, warm light against the damp stone walls.

ALEXIEL - stern

"The letters mentioned a tragedy, Barnaby. Tell me plainly what my uncle did to bring the house to this."

Barnaby stops near a wide archway and lifts his gnarled cane toward the ceiling, where a massive oak beam has cracked and sags dangerously low.

He then hobbles to a side door and swings it open to reveal a cramped room filled with the smell of old parchment and cold ash.

BARNABY - solemn, pointing

"He spent the harvest on maps and the winter on wine, my Lady. That ceiling is held up by prayer alone, and the ledger on the desk shows exactly what those prayers cost us."

Turn 2

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Continue

The study is cramped and smells of stale air and ancient dust.

Barnaby leans his weight against the edge of a heavy oak desk, the wood groaning under his slight frame. He uses his cane to push aside a pile of loose papers, revealing a thick, leather-bound book with edges worn smooth by frequent use.

BARNABY - solemn

"He didn't just drink the vintage, my Lady. He became obsessed with a lost ruin in the south—some heap of stones the locals call the Shattered Spire."

BARNABY - solemn

"He was convinced it held the key to a second sun."

He taps the ledger with the tip of his cane, the sound sharp in the quiet room. A layer of dust jumps from the leather cover.

BARNABY - bitter

"He drained the coffers to fund three separate expeditions. Sold the cattle, then the silver, then the family's favor at court."

BARNABY - bitter

"They found nothing but sand and fever. The third group never even came back to collect their final pay."

The old man flips the cover open. The pages are filled with tight, frantic handwriting, much of it blotted with ink or stained by spilled wine.

Columns of numbers march down the paper, each one ending in a zero that looks like a hollow eye.

BARNABY

"Lady Ashbury knows this. She watched him crumble, waiting for the moment the vine died on the branch."

BARNABY

"Now she offers fifty florins for the whole estate, vineyard and all. It is an insult, but without a single copper to pay the King's taxes, I cannot stop her from taking it by decree."

He looks at Alexiel, his hands trembling as they rest on the desk. Outside, the wind whistles through a gap in the window frame, a thin, lonely sound that emphasizes the emptiness of the house.

BARNABY - earnest

"The Willowford folk haven't seen a Tethlar in years. They've stopped looking to the manor for help and started looking to the road for a way out."

BARNABY - earnest

"If we don't show them something soon, there won't be anyone left to work the fields even if we find the coin."

A loose sheet of paper slides off the desk, fluttering to the floor. It is a map of the local area, with Riverside Manor circled in red ink and the word "HOPELESS" scrawled across the vineyard in a shaky hand.

ALEXIEL - cold

"My uncle was a fool who chased ghosts while his house burned. I have no intention of following his lead."

ALEXIEL - cold

"Show me the current debt, Barnaby. I want to know exactly how much it costs to silence a neighbor like Ashbury."

Barnaby turns a few more pages, his finger tracing a long list of outstanding debts to local merchants and the regional tax collector. The total at the bottom of the page is circled several times in red.

BARNABY

"Three hundred florins for the back taxes, and another hundred for the creditors in town. That is the price of keeping the doors on their hinges, my Lady."

BARNABY

"After that... we would still be standing in a ruin."

Turn 3

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: How can we make money

Barnaby lets out a long, rattling sigh as he looks at the red ink on the page.

He wipes his brow with a stained sleeve, his gaze shifting from the ledger to the ornate staff in Alexiel's hand.

ALEXIEL

"How can we make money, Barnaby? I will not see this house sold for a pittance to a woman who smells blood in the water."

BARNABY - weary, leaning on desk

"There are ways, my Lady, though none of them are easy or particularly dignified. First, there is the family silver."

BARNABY - weary, leaning on desk

"It is a set of twelve, buried in a lead box beneath the pantry floor. It would fetch a hundred florins at the market in Willowford tomorrow, but once it is gone, our table will be as bare as our pride."

He moves to the window, looking out over the tangled mess of the hillside. The vines are so thick with briars they look like a wall of green spears.

BARNABY

"Then there is the vineyard itself. If you can clear the thorns and show the tax collector that a harvest is coming, I might be able to negotiate a stay on the debts."

BARNABY

"A viable estate is harder to seize than a graveyard. It is back-breaking work, and the villagers will need to be convinced to return to the slope."

The old steward pauses, his voice dropping to a low, conspiratorial rasp. He gestures vaguely toward the floorboards beneath their feet.

BARNABY

"Finally, there is the matter of the cellar. I have heard noises in the night—heavy boots and the clink of glass."

BARNABY

"I suspect a Hand from the Thieves' Guild is using our river access to move contraband, likely life-stone or spirits. They think the house is empty."

BARNABY

"If you were to... convince them that a mistress has returned, they might be persuaded to pay a significant toll for their continued use of our tunnels."

He turns back to her, his expression grim. He looks at the fine rings on her fingers and then at the crumbling plaster of the study walls.

BARNABY

"One is a sacrifice, one is a struggle, and one is a gamble with dangerous men. Which path fits a Tethlar?"

A floorboard creaks in the hallway outside the study, sharp and sudden. Barnaby freezes, his hand tightening on his cane as he tilts his head toward the door.

Turn 4

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Go talk to smugglers in a friendly way

Barnaby lifts a finger to his lips, his eyes wide as the floorboard in the hall settles with a final, lingering groan.

He points his cane toward the door and then gestures toward the back of the house, indicating the service passage that leads toward the kitchens.

BARNABY - whispering

"The pantry. The stairs to the cellar are just behind the grain bin."

BARNABY - whispering

"If they are here, that is where they will emerge."

Alexiel moves with a quiet, predatory grace, her staff held low as she slips out of the study and down the narrow servant's corridor. The kitchens are cold, the twin hearths filled with gray ash that hasn't seen a flame in weeks.

Near the heavy wood of the walk-in pantry, a shadow detaches itself from the wall.

A man in a stained leather jerkin stands by the dumbwaiter, a short, jagged blade held loosely in one hand. He has a sack of clinking glass at his feet and looks more annoyed than surprised to see a noblewoman in his workspace.

ALEXIEL - calm

"Lower your steel. This house has a mistress again, and I prefer my kitchens without the scent of unwashed thieves."

ALEXIEL - calm

"I believe we have business to discuss regarding my cellar."

The thief tilts his head, his gaze flickering over Alexiel's fine silks and the glowing gem of her staff. He doesn't put the knife away, but he doesn't step forward either.

THIEF

"A mistress? This pile of rocks hasn't had a soul in it but the old man for years."

THIEF

"We use the tunnel because it's quiet."

ALEXIEL - stern

"It is quiet because I allowed it to be. If you wish to keep using my river access for your trade, you will find it much more profitable to pay for the privilege than to find the tunnels collapsed or the Watch informed."

(Persuasion Check: Result 29 vs DC 25 - Success)

[Roll: 5 (d20) + 8 (Charisma) + 10 (Persuasion) + 6 (Noble Presence) = 29]

The man let out a short, dry laugh. He kicks the sack of bottles, and the sound of glass on glass fills the room.

He was ready to scoff, but the weight of Alexiel's authority—and the clear threat behind her eyes—makes him hesitate.

THIEF

"You talk big for a girl with one servant and a sagging roof. The Guild doesn't pay for what it can take, but..."

THIEF

"I'm not looking for a fight with a witch today. Tell you what."

THIEF

"You stay upstairs, we keep the tunnel, and maybe I don't tell the boys to clear you out of your own bed."

He reaches into a pouch at his belt and tosses a small, heavy purse onto the scarred wooden butcher's block between them. It lands with a dull thud of copper and a few clinking silver coins.

THIEF

"That's fifty florins. Call it a gift for the housewarming."

THIEF

"Don't expect it every week unless you can offer us something better than a damp floor."

He scoops up his sack and retreats toward the pantry door, his eyes never leaving Alexiel until he vanishes into the gloom of the cellar stairs. Barnaby hobbles into the kitchen a moment later, his face pale as he looks at the purse on the table.

BARNABY - shaky

"Fifty florins? It is a start, my Lady, but they will be back."

BARNABY - shaky

"Men like that don't pay out of the goodness of their hearts. They're testing the fence to see if it bites."

Turn 5

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Call after the thief. Ask

Alexiel strides toward the open pantry door, her staff tapping against the cold stone floor as she calls out to the retreating figure.

ALEXIEL

"Wait. If your Guild values this tunnel, I would know who oversees your Trade in these parts."

ALEXIEL

"Who is your master?"

The thief pauses on the top step, his silhouette framed by the darkness of the stairwell. He looks back over his shoulder, a thin smile visible in the dim light of the kitchen.

THIEF - wry

"Masters are for dogs and peasants, Lady. If you want to talk business, find Daran at the Broken Cask in Willowford."

THIEF - wry

"He is the Hand for the Silverflow."

He descends quickly, the sound of his footsteps swallowed by the shadows below. Barnaby reaches out, his hand trembling as he gestures toward the open doorway where the thief disappeared.

BARNABY - anxious

"He is gone, my Lady. Do not follow him."

BARNABY - anxious

"The cellar is a labyrinth of rotted storage and river-seep. Pursuing a man like that into the dark without a lantern would be a fool's errand."

The old steward turns back to the butcher's block and picks up the heavy purse the thief left behind. He lets the coins clink inside the leather, his face drawn and pale in the morning light.

BARNABY

"Fifty florins. It is a start, but they will be back."

BARNABY

"We have taken their coin, and now they will think they own the floor beneath our feet. We have bought ourselves a day, but I fear we have also bought a master we did not want."

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Ask him of any other ways to make money. Any mercenary jobs?

Alexiel turns away from the cellar stairs, her gaze fixed on the heavy purse of coins resting on the butcher's block.

ALEXIEL - stern

"Fifty florins is a drop in a very deep well. Tell me, Barnaby, are there other ways to find coin?"

ALEXIEL - stern

"Are there mercenary jobs or contracts in the village that require a firm hand?"

Barnaby carefully sets the purse on the scarred wood, his hand trembling as he leans his weight onto his gnarled cane. He gestures vaguely through the kitchen window toward the cluster of thatched roofs in the valley below.

BARNABY - wry

"I know little of the sellsword's trade, my Lady, but the village likely has tasks for a capable hand. The miller's storehouse is plagued by giant rats, and he offers a bounty for their hides."

BARNABY - wry

"The village council also seeks someone to clear the overgrown path to the old shrine to ensure safe passage for pilgrims. It is not the grand work of your ancestors, but it is honest coin."

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Go find daran the thief leader and see if he has jobs. Explain I’m a warlock

Barnaby grips his cane with both hands, the wood creaking under his weight as he stares at the cellar door.

He turns back to Alexiel, his brow furrowed with genuine concern.

BARNABY

"Seeking a man like Daran is a dangerous path, my Lady. Men of your bloodline have always traded in secrets, but the Thieves' Guild deals in blood and debt."

BARNABY

"If you truly mean to walk into that den, do not go on foot like a common traveler. Take the roan from the stables—she is old, but she is sturdy and will lend you some small measure of dignity on the road to Willowford."

Alexiel leaves the manor behind, the crumbling stone walls fading into the morning mist as she rides the aging mare down the winding hillside track.

The Silverflow River glitters to the side, a wide ribbon of blue cutting through the emerald fields of Highvale.

The nine-kilometer journey is quiet, the only sound the steady rhythm of hooves on packed dirt and the occasional cry of a hawk circling above the grain fields.

Willowford appears at the bend of the river, a cluster of stone cottages huddled together near the water's edge.

The Willow Ford Inn stands as the village's anchor, its whitewashed walls bright against the green of the common. Inside, the common room is cool and smells of stale ale and woodsmoke.

A few local farmers sit in the shadows, their hushed conversations dying away as the half-elf noblewoman enters. A man sits alone at a corner table, a scarred cup of cider in front of him and a heavy leather coat draped over his chair.

ALEXIEL

"You would be Daran. I am Alexiel of the Tethlar line, and I have come to discuss a more formal arrangement for the use of my cellar."

ALEXIEL

"I am a warlock, and I find our interests may align if you have work that requires a mistress of the arcane."

(Persuasion Check: Result 24 vs DC 20 - Success)

[Roll: 6 (d20) + 8 (Charisma) + 10 (Persuasion) = 24]

Daran doesn't look up at first, his fingers tracing a deep notch in the tabletop. When he finally raises his head, his eyes are sharp and calculating, devoid of the casual mockery the thief in the pantry had shown.

He takes a slow pull from his cider, leaning back as he surveys the tall, violet-eyed woman standing before him.

DARAN

"A warlock in a ruin. My man said you had a bit of iron in your spine, but he didn't mention the noble name."

DARAN

"Names are heavy things in Highvale, Lady Tethlar. They usually come with baggage that the Guild doesn't like to carry."

He taps a heavy ring against the wood, the sound rhythmic and sharp in the quiet room. He gestures to the empty stool across from him, his smile not quite reaching his eyes.

DARAN

"If you're looking for coin and aren't afraid to get those fine robes dusty, I might have a use for a woman of your talents. There's a shipment of lifestone that went missing near the old mill."

DARAN

"The miller claims he knows nothing, but the rats in his cellar have grown unnaturally large and aggressive. Clear the vermin and find my crates, and we can talk about a more..."

DARAN

"permanent lease on your tunnels."

Turn 8

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Go to the place and clear the rats

Daran drains the last of his cider, the wooden mug hitting the table with a dull thud that marks the end of the negotiation.

He leans forward, his shadow stretching long across the scarred wood toward Alexiel.

DARAN

"The miller is a nervous wreck named Hobb. You will find him just across the stone bridge."

DARAN

"If you bring back the crates stamped with the silver leaf, I will pay you fifty florins and we can discuss your cellar as a proper business arrangement."

Alexiel mounts her mare and follows the river road out of Willowford. The path narrows as the cultivated fields give way to high grass and thickets of bramble.

The air grows heavy with the scent of damp earth and rotting vegetation as the silver ribbon of the river churns into white foam near a crumbling stone bridge.

Beyond the bridge, the old mill stands like a grey ghost against the trees, its massive wheel frozen and choked with river weed.

Hobb the miller is waiting near the door of the mill house, wringing a grease-stained cap in his hands. He is a small, jittery man with flour-dusted clothes and eyes that dart toward the dark interior of the mill every time the floorboards groan.

HOBB

"You are here for the... the problem?"

HOBB

"They are beneath the floor, Lady. They have chewed through the grain sacks and now they bite at the very stone."

HOBB

"I cannot go in there. It is not right, how big they have grown."

Alexiel ignores the man's trembling and pushes past the heavy oak door. The interior of the mill house is a cavern of shadows and dust.

The air is thick with the smell of moldy grain and something sharper—the musky, biting scent of nesting vermin. A low, rhythmic scratching sound fills the space, coming from beneath the heavy planks of the floor.

Alexiel raises her staff, the dark gem at its head beginning to pulse with a sickly, violet light. She calls upon the cold power of her patron, weaving a strand of shadow that drips from her fingers like ink.

She lashes the darkness toward a cluster of holes in the wainscoting, intending to choke the life from the rats within.

The magic strikes, but the shadows don't spread as she intended. Instead of a silent death, the dark energy hits the wood with a loud crack, splintering the floorboards and sending a shower of sparks into the dust.

A chorus of high-pitched shrieks erupts from the darkness, and the scratching turns into a frantic, scratching scramble.

A swarm of rats, each the size of a small dog with fur matted by grease and filth, surges from the shadows. Their eyes catch the dying light of the spell, glowing a dull, hungry red.

They don't flee the magic; instead, they scatter across the room, hissing and baring yellowed incisors.

One of the larger rats lunges from the debris, its claws clicking on the wood as it leaps toward Alexiel's boots.

The dark magic has stirred the nest, but the creatures are more resilient and aggressive than anticipated, their forms twitching with an unnatural, frantic energy as they begin to circle her.

ALEXIEL - annoyed

"Disturbed, but not destroyed. Stand back, miller, unless you wish to be part of the feast."

Turn 9

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Use some aoe damage magic on the rats

Alexiel raises her wooden staff, her knuckles white against the grain.

She draws on the cold, hungry power of her pact, weaving a thick shroud of violet shadow between her palms. With a sharp gesture, she hurls the dark energy outward in a wide, sweeping arc that blankets the dusty floorboards.

The magic ripples through the air like ink in water, catching the three giant rats in a crushing wave of pressure.

The first and second rats are thrown backward against the mill machinery, their fur singed and their bodies bruised as they tumble through the dust.

The largest rat, scarred and massive, takes the brunt of the blow on its shoulder but barely breaks its stride, its hide only slightly blackened by the dark force.

The two smaller rats recover instantly, driven by a frantic, magical hunger. Rat 1 darts through the dust, its claws clicking on the wood before it sinks its yellow teeth deep into Alexiel’s ankle.

Rat 2 follows a heartbeat later, lunging from the side to clamp its jaws onto her calf, the sharp teeth tearing through her linen robes and drawing a sudden spray of red.

ALEXIEL - grimacing

"Filthy creatures... back!"

The scarred alpha rat rears back on its haunches, its mouth opening to reveal rows of jagged, yellowed bone.

It lets out a piercing, glass-shattering shriek that vibrates through the very timber of the mill, a call that is answered by a hundred tiny scrapings from deep within the walls.

Before the echo dies, it springs from a stack of flour sacks, its heavy weight slamming into Alexiel as its teeth sink viciously into her thigh.

Blood soaks into the noblewoman's robes as she staggers under the combined weight of the pack, her breath catching at the sharp, burning pain.

Hobb scrambles backward toward the open doorway, his face pale and his boots slipping on the flour-slicked floor.

HOBB - panicked, shouting

"Mind the grain! My Lady, the sacks!"

HOBB - panicked, shouting

"If they tear, I'm ruined—watch your feet, they're everywhere!"

The miller clutches his cap to his chest, his eyes wide as he watches the dark magic and the blood from the safety of the threshold. The rats do not let go, their red eyes fixed on Alexiel as they pull and snap at her legs.

w_chevron_right

Alexiel: Use a cc spell on the rats to make them helpless and cast shield on myself

Alexiel slams the butt of her staff into the floorboards, the impact vibrating through the stone foundation.

A suffocating chill bleeds from her palms, forming a shimmering, translucent barrier of violet light that wraps around her like a second skin.

As the rats prepare to lunge again, she gestures with her free hand, pulling a heavy, cloying fog of darkness from the shadows of the rafters and pinning the vermin to the floor.

The magical weight is sudden and absolute. The rats shriek as the shadow coils around them like thick cords, pressing their bellies into the wood and holding their snapping jaws shut.

The scarred alpha thrashes, its powerful muscles bulging beneath its grease-slicked hide, but the dark magic holds firm, leaving the entire pack twitching and helpless in the gloom.

ALEXIEL - stern

"Be still. You are fodder for the void, nothing more."

Hobb scrambles further toward the light of the doorway, his boots kicking up a cloud of flour as he trips over a discarded scoop.

He points a trembling finger at a stack of burlap sacks piled precariously against a grinding stone, his eyes darting between the trapped rats and the encroaching violet fog.

HOBB - panicked, shouting

"The grain, Lady! Watch the grain!"

HOBB - panicked, shouting

"If your dark fire touches those sacks, there won't be enough flour left in Highvale to bake a single crust! Don't ruin me while you're killing them!"

The mill wheel groans outside, a slow, rhythmic reminder of the river's power. Inside, the only sounds are the frantic, muffled chittering of the bound rats and the sharp, jagged breaths of the miller.

The lifestone crates remain hidden somewhere in the deepening shadows of the cellar below.

Alexiel: Make sure grain isn’t burned and then carefully slit the throats of each rats they should be helpless so should be easy

Alexiel lowers her center of gravity, her boots finding purchase on the flour-dusted floorboards.

She keeps her violet-hued magic contained, pressing down on the squirming vermin with a heavy, unseen weight that keeps the shadows away from the dry grain sacks.

She draws her iron dagger from her belt, the blade catching a sliver of light from a high window as she drops to one knee.

She reaches for the first rat, her blade aimed at its exposed throat. The creature, sensing the cold steel, gives a desperate, muscular lunge.

Its slick fur slides through the shadowy bonds like grease, and its neck twists away at an impossible angle just as the dagger strikes the wood.

Alexiel snatches at the second rat, but it thrashes with a sudden, violent spasm that sends it rolling toward a pile of empty husks, the blade missing its mark by a fraction of an inch.

The scarred alpha rat lets out a muffled, guttural growl, its heavy chest heaving against the violet fog. As Alexiel lunges for its throat, the massive rodent uses its sheer bulk to roll onto its back.

Its thick, yellowed claws lash out in a frantic blur, forcing her to pull her hand back to avoid a deep gouge. The magical tether holds them fast to the floor, but their bodies move with a panicked, instinctive speed that defies her simple execution.

Hobb peers out from behind a thick cedar support beam, his face a mask of sweating terror. He watches the silver blade flash near the delicate burlap of the grain sacks, his fingers digging into the rough wood of the pillar.

HOBB - shouting, panicked

"Mind the edge! If you nick the weave, the whole year's profit bleeds out onto the floor!"

HOBB - shouting, panicked

"Watch the blood, Lady—don't let it soak the rye!"

The rats continue to struggle, their red eyes fixed on the dagger with a primitive, calculating intelligence.

The mill house remains a stalemate of shimmering magic and snapping teeth, the crates of lifestone still buried somewhere beneath the frantic scratching of the nest.

Alexiel: Tighten shadow binding to choke them to death

Alexiel clenches her hand, drawing the violet coils tight around the throats of the three beasts.

The shadows constrict like hemp rope, but as the rats begin to struggle, Alexiel’s patience snaps. She isn't here to toy with vermin.

She channels a sudden, violent surge of Tethlar power through her staff. The violet light flares into a blinding, obsidian darkness that fills the mill house.

The coils don't just squeeze; they implode. There is a series of sickening crunches as the shadows crush the life from the rats instantly, snuffing out their red eyes before they can even shriek.

(Magic Attack: Result 34 vs DC 10 - Critical Success)

[Roll: 20 (d20) + 9 (Intelligence) + 5 (Arcane) = 34]

The alpha rat’s head is snapped back, its heavy body thrown against the grinding stone where it falls limp and lifeless. The other two rats are crumpled like discarded rags across the floorboards.

The oppressive weight of the shadow magic lingers for a moment before dissipating into the dusty air of the mill.

HOBB - breathless, stunned

"By the gods... I've never seen anything like that."

HOBB - breathless, stunned

"They’re... they're all dead."

Hobb steps out from behind the beam, his eyes wide as he looks at the charred floorboards where the violet magic struck. He quickly looks toward the window, checking for the Silverflow guards, but the street outside remains quiet.

The threat is gone, and the path to the cellar—and the missing lifestone crates—is now clear.

Alexiel: Continue

HOBB - shaking, nodding

"Well. That was..."

HOBB - shaking, nodding

"efficient. A bit messy for the rye, maybe, but efficient."

HOBB - shaking, nodding

"I suppose I owe you a debt of thanks for keeping my throat intact, my Lady."

The miller scurries across the floor, his boots making a soft scratching sound on the wood as he avoids the patches of violet-scorched timber.

He reaches the heavy oak trapdoor near the back of the room, pulling a ring of iron keys from his belt with trembling fingers.

The lock clicks open with a heavy, metallic thud that echoes in the rafters.

HOBB - straining, pulling

"The crates Daran wants... they're down here."

Days bleed into weeks as the summer sun bakes the Highvale soil. At Riverside Manor, the air grows heavy with the scent of ripening grapes and the metallic tang of the hidden lifestone trade.

Barnaby moves through the halls like a ghost, managing the new guards and keeping the books clean while the village of Willowford holds its collective breath.

Reports come in from the southern bend—fines levied, permits revoked, and Miller Hobb’s wagons held for "safety inspections" until his grain begins to spoil in the heat.

The solstice arrives with a parade of polished breastplates and snapping banners as the High Commissioner’s carriage rattles into the village square. For three days, the village lockup is a hive of activity.

Aurelian Strand is seen everywhere, his uniform brushed and his back straight, leading the King’s accountants through a labyrinth of backdated receipts and forged registries provided by the Tethlar estate.

On the fourth night, a young boy in a stained tunic sprints up the drive of Riverside Manor, clutching a crumpled bit of parchment sealed with a blob of dirty red wax.

He hands it to Barnaby at the gate and vanishes back into the trees before the guards can even challenge him.

Barnaby enters the solar where Alexiel waits, his face unreadable. He breaks the seal and reads the single line of cramped, shaky handwriting aloud.

The audit is over; the Commissioner found the books "unusually orderly" and has moved on to Ashbury Hall, leaving the Captain with a formal commendation for his diligence in a difficult year.

BARNABY

"It seems our investment has borne fruit, mistress. The Captain is now a hero in the eyes of the Crown, and Miller Hobb is currently petitioning the village council for a loan to cover his new "sanitation fines." The river is ours in all but name."

The victory is quiet, marked only by the clink of florins being sorted in the strongbox and the distant, rhythmic thud of the mill wheel slowing down for lack of work.

In the village, people speak in low voices about the sudden change in the Captain’s fortune, but no one dares ask where the gold came from.

The Silverflow continues to churn, carrying its secrets toward the sea.
